### 2.2 What it looks, feels and smells like

- **The jar [M: images]:** a round pink frosted tub, with a pink, clear, wobbly gel essence that drips off the pads like jelly.
- **The pad [M: image `Luxe_GelPad_Imagev5`]:** a 7 cm round (about the size of the bottom of a coffee mug [R]). Two sides:
  - **"Soft & Hydrating Gel Side: Peptide-Infused Fabric, Hydration Delivery with Mask Pack Effect"**
  - **"Gentle Plain Side: Low-irritation Vegan Fabric, Lock-in Moisture after wiping"**
- **Which side is which is confusing [M].** Anua (same pad) describes it the other way round: *"The peptide-infused fabric side works as a gentle pore minimizer... while the smooth gel side delivers soothing hydration"* (anua.us product JSON). Its description also says the pads *"gently exfoliate dead skin cells"*, which can only mean the light rub of the textured fabric, since there's no acid. A Cult Beauty reviewer of the Anua pad found the two-sided pad confusing [M: earlier report 6.2]. **Practical tip [R]:** the side that feels slippery and jelly-like is the mask side; the side that feels like fabric is the wiping side.
- **Feel on the skin:** no reviews of the Luxe pads were found anywhere. For the identical Anua pad, buyers say: *"These pads are super saturated with quality skin care ingredients that cool the skin"* (Jennifer S., age 51–55, dry and sensitive skin, 5 stars, anua.com via Okendo, 9 Jun 2026; **marked "incentivized" by the review system**, meaning she got something for reviewing) [M: `data/reviews/toner-pads/anua-us-glow-pad-okendo-rendered-reviews-2026-10-31.json`]; *"leaves the skin nice and hydrated"*; *"the packaging takes up a lot of space"* (Cult Beauty, earlier report 6.2) [M]; and *"It hydrates my skin, calms redness and it's pefect under makeup"* (Lucia, Cult Beauty, 2026-04-14, 5 stars; review A-012) [M]. Gel pads in general can feel sticky: *"Just wish it wasn't so sticky!!"* (@AhnaHendrix, on a different pad, EmpVHOGyYLw) [M].
- **Smell:** no fragrance is listed [M: INCI]. For the identical Anua pad, buyers say *"Pads are very saturated, there’s next to no smell"* (Vitacost, 2026-05-22, 4 stars, review A-041) and *"A few people mention a light smell from the pad but I did not notice any scent"* (Ulta, most-helpful positive review, review A-059) [M]. Another Vitacost buyer adds that it *"does have a slight bitter taste if it accidentally gets on your lips"* (2026-06-13, 5 stars, review A-036) [M]. So: almost no smell. No review of the Luxe jar itself was found.

### 2.3 Every ingredient, one by one

**First, how to read an ingredient list.** In the US, ingredients *"must be listed in descending order of predominance"*, but *"Ingredients present at a concentration not exceeding 1% may be listed in any order"* [M: FDA Cosmetics Labeling Guide]. So the first few ingredients are most of the product, and everything after roughly the first 6–10 is usually under 1% [R]. The check found that Anua's own website lists the same 45 ingredients in a slightly different order from its packaging image, which shows how loose the order is below 1% [M: check file section 2].

**The list on the Luxe image** (45 ingredients, identical to Anua's) [M: `luxe-pads-inci-transcribed-from-image-2026-10-31.txt`; `luxe-vs-anua-inci-comparison-2026-10-31.txt`]:

> Aqua (Water), Butylene Glycol, Glycereth-26, Niacinamide, Glycerin, 1,2-Hexanediol, Diethoxyethyl Succinate, Hydroxyethyl Urea, Hydroxyacetophenone, Glycereth-25 PCA Isostearate, Glycosyl Trehalose, Hydrogenated Starch Hydrolysate, Melia Azadirachta Leaf Extract, Ethylhexylglycerin, Xanthan Gum, Propanediol, Adenosine, Melia Azadirachta Flower Extract, Coccinia Indica Fruit Extract, Disodium EDTA, Sodium DNA, Hydrolyzed Collagen, Aloe Barbadensis Flower Extract, Solanum Melongena (Eggplant) Fruit Extract, Coptis Japonica Root Extract, Ocimum Sanctum Leaf Extract, Zea Mays (Corn) Kernel Extract, Corallina Officinalis Extract, Curcuma Longa (Turmeric) Root Extract, Sodium Hyaluronate, Hyaluronic Acid, Hydrolyzed Hyaluronic Acid, Terminalia Ferdinandiana Fruit Extract, Fructan, Glucose, Lecithin, Acetyl Glutamine, Collagen Extract, Bacillus/Soybean Ferment Extract, Caprylyl Glycol, Folic Acid, Hexapeptide-11, Oligopeptide-1, Oligopeptide-2, Oligopeptide-3

The only amounts published anywhere (by Anua, for the same formula): **sodium DNA 100 ppm, sodium hyaluronate 5.002 ppm, hyaluronic acid 5 ppm, hydrolyzed hyaluronic acid 5 ppm** [M: dodoskin.com]. Every other amount is **not found**.

Below, the 45 are grouped by job. The number is the position in the list.

#### Group A. The base: water and the main water-grabbers (most of the jar)

| # | Ingredient | What it is, plainly | What it does here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Aqua (Water)** | Purified water | The bulk of the gel. Everything else is dissolved in it [R] |
| 2 | **Butylene Glycol** | A light, clear liquid related to glycerin, but less sticky | Humectant (holds water) and solvent (a liquid that other things dissolve in, so it helps plant extracts dissolve). Gives "slip" [R] |
| 3 | **Glycereth-26** | Glycerin chemically joined to a short chain of a common polymer (a polymer is a long, chain-like molecule; here it is polyethylene glycol, a common cosmetic ingredient; the full name is "a polyethylene glycol ether of glycerin") | A humectant and moisturiser that feels less tacky than plain glycerin [M: supplier datasheets via search, "acts as a solvent, moisturizer and humectant"] |
| 5 | **Glycerin** | The classic moisturiser, made from plant oils; also in soap and sweets | One of the most proven humectants. Pulls water into the top layer [R] |
| 15 | **Xanthan Gum** | A thickener made by fermenting sugar; also used in salad dressing and gluten-free baking | Turns the liquid into the wobbly gel you see [R] |
| 16 | **Propanediol** | A light liquid, often made from corn sugar | Humectant and solvent [R] |

**In plain words [R]:** the first five ingredients (water, three water-grabbers and niacinamide) are almost certainly most of the jar. This is a water-and-glycerin gel.

#### Group B. The "doing" ingredients, the ones on the marketing

| # | Ingredient | What it is | What it does, and how strong the evidence is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | **Niacinamide** | Vitamin B3 | **The most important real active in the pad [R].** In a 12-week split-face trial on 50 women aged 40–60, a moisturiser with 5% niacinamide improved fine lines and wrinkles, dark spots, texture, red blotchiness and yellowing compared with the same moisturiser without it (Bissett 2004, *Int J Cosmet Sci*, PMID 18492135) [M]. In 18 people with dark spots, 5% niacinamide *"significantly decreased hyperpigmentation and increased skin lightness"* in 4 weeks (Hakozaki 2002, *Br J Dermatol*, PMID 12100180) [M]. It also helps skin make its own ceramides (Tanno 2000, *Br J Dermatol*, via search) [M, secondary]. **Amount in the pad: not stated.** Being 4th, above glycerin, suggests a few percent [R]. Dr Dray calls it *"an evidence-based ingredient for dark spots, redness, the moisture barrier, anti-aging"* [M: XC2OZDLpEJ0]. Can briefly flush or sting very reactive skin [R] |
| 17 | **Adenosine** | A natural molecule found in every cell | Korea lists it as a "functional cosmetic" anti-wrinkle ingredient, usually at **0.04%** [M: secondary sources only; the official MFDS list was not pulled]. One randomised trial (people were put into groups by chance, like a coin toss, so the test is fair): 126 women aged 45–65, adenosine cream twice a day, smoother crow's-feet skin at 3 and 8 weeks (Abella 2006, L'Oréal, PMID 18489289) [M: from `03-under-eye-balm.md`]. **Amount in the pad: not stated** [M] |
| 21 | **Sodium DNA (PDRN)** | DNA fragments from salmon | The headline ingredient. **100 ppm (0.01%)** in the identical Anua formula [M]. On the skin surface it works as a light moisturiser; the "repair" story is not shown for creams or pads (section 1.7) [M: Dermatology Times; Dr Dray]. **Fish source: allergy warning** |
| 30 | **Sodium Hyaluronate** | The salt form of hyaluronic acid (HA), a water-holding sugar chain | Humectant, film on the surface. **5 ppm** [M]. For scale: a 60-day trial of 0.1% HA creams (76 women aged 30–60; the creams were used around the eyes, which the paper calls "periocular") improved hydration and elasticity, and the smaller-sized HA reduced wrinkle depth (Pavicic 2011, *J Drugs Dermatol*, PMID 22052267) [M]. The pad has **about 200 times less** of each form (5 ppm = 0.0005%) [M: calculation] |
| 31 | **Hyaluronic Acid** | The acid form of HA | Same as above, 5 ppm [M] |
| 32 | **Hydrolyzed Hyaluronic Acid** | HA chopped into smaller pieces | Smaller pieces may get further into the top layer [M: Pavicic 2011 suggests better penetration for low-molecular-weight HA]. 5 ppm [M] |
| 22 | **Hydrolyzed Collagen** | Collagen protein broken into small pieces; source not stated (could be fish, pig or cow) | A humectant and film-former (it leaves a thin, invisible layer on the skin). **It does not become your collagen.** Doctorly on a collagen cream: *"I think they use collagen cuz it's a buzzword. It's not going to penetrate your skin. It could be a good moisturizer"* [M: 1NOgLh46HdM]. A viewer: *"collagen cannot be absorbed through the skin, so the collagen is not what's soothing your skin"* (@jbeandip, 103 likes) [M] |
| 38 | **Collagen Extract** | Another collagen ingredient; source not stated | As above. The Luxe image calls it "Patented 7D vita collagen" [M] |
| 42 | **Hexapeptide-11** | A peptide (6 amino acids), originally from fermented yeast [R] | Anua's claim: *"Hexapeptide-11 provides skin elasticity"* [M: dodoskin Anua description]. Human evidence at this level: not found. Near the end of the list, so a tiny amount [R] |
| 43 | **Oligopeptide-1** | On Korean labels, the name for a lab-made copy of **EGF (epidermal growth factor)**, a 53-amino-acid human protein; officially "sh-Oligopeptide-1" [M: search results, ingredient databases] | Anua's claim: *"provides collagen synthesis"* [M]. Evidence is limited and small [M: search summary]. Tiny amount [R] |
| 44 | **Oligopeptide-2** | A lab-made peptide; on Korean labels often a copy of the growth factor IGF-1 [R, not confirmed for this product] | Anua's claim: *"provides skin anti-aging"* [M]. Tiny amount |
| 45 | **Oligopeptide-3** | A lab-made peptide; exact identity on this label **not found** | Anua's claim: *"provides moisturizing effect"* [M]. Tiny amount |

#### Group C. Extra moisture-holders and texture helpers

| # | Ingredient | What it is | What it does here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | **Diethoxyethyl Succinate** | A light, water-friendly liquid | Solvent and skin-feel helper, used to make watery products feel silky and to carry other ingredients into the top layer [R; no source found today] |
| 8 | **Hydroxyethyl Urea** | A relative of urea, the moisturiser in many foot creams, but gentler | Humectant and skin conditioner; judged safe by the US Cosmetic Ingredient Review panel *"when formulated to be non-irritating"* [M: CIR and COSMILE Europe via search] |
| 10 | **Glycereth-25 PCA Isostearate** | A glycerin-based molecule joined to a fatty acid | Emulsifier/solubiliser (a helper that lets oil and water mix): helps tiny amounts of oily ingredients dissolve in the water gel, and softens [R] |
| 11 | **Glycosyl Trehalose** | A sugar (trehalose, found in mushrooms and yeast) with extra sugar attached | EU ingredient database (CosIng) lists "Emulsion Stabilising, Film Forming" [M: via SkinSort in search] |
| 12 | **Hydrogenated Starch Hydrolysate** | A sugar-alcohol syrup made from starch | Humectant [M: SkinSort via search] |
| 34 | **Fructan** | A chain of fruit sugars (like inulin) | Humectant, light film [R] |
| 35 | **Glucose** | Ordinary sugar | Humectant [R] |
| 37 | **Acetyl Glutamine** | A form of the amino acid glutamine | Skin-conditioning, moisturising [R] |

#### Group D. Plant, sea and ferment extracts (all in small amounts)

Eight of these (neem leaf and flower, ivy gourd, aloe flower, eggplant, holy basil, coral weed, turmeric) look like a ready-made blend of mostly Indian (Ayurvedic) plants plus a seaweed that suppliers sell as one package; such blends are common in Korean formulas [R]. Each is likely well under 1% [R].

**"Antioxidant"** comes up a lot below. An antioxidant is an ingredient that helps protect the skin from damage by sunlight and air pollution, a bit like the way lemon juice stops a cut apple going brown [R]. At these tiny amounts, the protection is mostly a lab claim.

| # | Ingredient | What it is | What it's there for |
|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | **Melia Azadirachta Leaf Extract** | **Neem** leaf, an Indian tree used in traditional medicine. (dodoskin's English list mistranslates it as "indigo plant") [M: comparison file] | Antioxidant and soothing claims; mostly lab data [R] |
| 18 | **Melia Azadirachta Flower Extract** | Neem flower | As above [R] |
| 19 | **Coccinia Indica Fruit Extract** | Ivy gourd, a small Indian vegetable | Antioxidant claims [R] |
| 23 | **Aloe Barbadensis Flower Extract** | Aloe vera **flower** (not the gel from the leaf) | Soothing claims [R] |
| 24 | **Solanum Melongena (Eggplant) Fruit Extract** | Aubergine | Antioxidant claims [R] |
| 25 | **Coptis Japonica Root Extract** | Goldthread root, a Japanese/Chinese herb rich in berberine | Antibacterial and anti-inflammatory in lab tests [R] |
| 26 | **Ocimum Sanctum Leaf Extract** | Holy basil (tulsi) | Antioxidant, soothing claims [R] |
| 27 | **Zea Mays (Corn) Kernel Extract** | Corn | Moisturising, skin-conditioning [R]. **Corn allergy note** |
| 28 | **Corallina Officinalis Extract** | A red seaweed ("coral weed"); dodoskin's "coral lily" is a mistranslation [M: comparison file] | Mineral-rich, skin-conditioning [R] |
| 29 | **Curcuma Longa (Turmeric) Root Extract** | Turmeric | Antioxidant claims. At this level it won't stain [R] |
| 33 | **Terminalia Ferdinandiana Fruit Extract** | **Kakadu plum**, an Australian fruit naturally rich in vitamin C | Antioxidant. **It is not a "vitamin C derivative"** as the Luxe page claims; it's a fruit extract near the bottom of the list [M: check file 4b] |
| 39 | **Bacillus/Soybean Ferment Extract** | Soybeans fermented with Bacillus bacteria (the natto process); trade names include Cellplus+ and Actigym | Skin conditioning and antioxidant [M: SkinSort and Paula's Choice via search]. **Soy allergy note** |
| 36 | **Lecithin** | A fatty substance usually from soy, sunflower or egg; source not stated | Emulsifier; can form tiny bubbles that carry ingredients [R]. Possible soy source |
| 41 | **Folic Acid** | Vitamin B9 | Antioxidant claims; tiny amount [R] |

#### Group E. The "keep it fresh and stable" team

A water gel full of sugars is a feast for germs. These stop that [R].

| # | Ingredient | What it is | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | **1,2-Hexanediol** | A light glycol | Humectant that also helps preserve; very common in Korean products [R] |
| 9 | **Hydroxyacetophenone** | A small aromatic molecule | Antioxidant [M: supplier listing via search]; widely used as a preservative booster (it helps the preservatives keep germs out, so less of them is needed) and soothing agent [R] |
| 14 | **Ethylhexylglycerin** | A glycerin relative | Preservative booster and skin conditioner; a rare cause of contact allergy [R] |
| 20 | **Disodium EDTA** | A "chelator" | Grabs stray metal traces (from water or plant extracts) so the formula doesn't go off or change colour. Kitchen picture: like a water softener [R] |
| 40 | **Caprylyl Glycol** | A glycol | Humectant and preservative booster [R] |

#### What's NOT in it [M: INCI]

| Not in it | Why that matters |
|---|---|
| Exfoliating acids (AHA, BHA, PHA, including the "maltobionic acid" the page claims) | Gentle, but it won't resurface or unclog pores. No "purging" |
| Retinol or any retinoid | No peeling; but no proven collagen-building either |
| Vitamin C or a vitamin C derivative (despite the page claim) | No brightening from vitamin C |
| Fragrance or essential oils | Kinder to sensitive skin |
| Drying alcohol (ethanol, "alcohol denat.") | Not drying |
| Oils, butters, ceramides | It doesn't seal moisture in. Use a moisturiser after |
| Centella (a soothing herb, also called "cica" or tiger grass) | Not a "cica" pad |

#### What the 45 ingredients add up to [R]

- **About 80–90% of what you put on your face is water and water-grabbers** (estimate from list order; amounts not published).
- **One real workhorse:** niacinamide, at an unknown but probably meaningful level.
- **One modest extra:** adenosine (unknown level).
- **The headline ingredients are tiny:** PDRN 0.01%, each hyaluronic acid 0.0005%, peptides and collagen near the end.
- **A rough per-pad guess:** 200 g ÷ 140 pads ≈ 1.4 g per pad, fabric included [M: calculation]. At 100 ppm, that is **at most about 0.14 mg of PDRN per pad** [R: assumes Anua's level and counts the fabric as liquid, so the real figure is lower].

#### Allergy and sensitivity flags [M: INCI; R: advice]

| Ingredient | Allergy |
|---|---|
| Sodium DNA (salmon) | **Fish** |
| Hydrolyzed collagen, collagen extract (source not stated) | Possibly **fish**, or pork/beef |
| Bacillus/soybean ferment; lecithin (source not stated) | **Soy** |
| Corn kernel extract; propanediol (often corn-derived) | Corn (rare) |
| Neem, turmeric, holy basil, coptis, aloe | Plant sensitivities (rare) |
| Ethylhexylglycerin | Rare contact allergy |
| Niacinamide | Can cause brief flushing or tingling on reactive skin [R] |

### 2.4 The claims it makes, and what the evidence says

All quotes from luxeresearchlab.com/products/luxe-pads, pulled 2026-10-31 [M]. Verdicts are my reading [R] unless a source is named.

| Claim | Honest verdict |
|---|---|
| "Better-Looking Skin in 30 Days or You Don't Pay" | A money-back offer, not evidence |
| "Most women feel a difference after the very first use — skin feels smoother, cleaner, and more hydrated immediately" | **Believable.** Hydration shows within minutes |
| "By week 2, texture starts to refine and a subtle glow appears" | **Plausible** with daily hydration |
| "By week 4–8, dark spots fade, fine lines soften, and firmness improves visibly" | **Partly.** Niacinamide can soften the look of spots and fine lines over 4–12 weeks in studies at 5% (amount here unknown). "Firmness improves" is not supported |
| "PDRN (99% purity) — the regenerative ingredient Korean dermatologists use in-clinic" | **Misleading.** Clinics inject it; the pad holds about 0.01%; "99%" is the raw material's purity |
| "PDRN targets the cellular repair your skin needs now" / "PDRN rebuilds underneath" | **Not supported** for creams or pads (Dermatology Times; Dr Dray) |
| "No retinol. No harsh acids. No synthetic fragrances. No drying alcohols." | **True** per the ingredient list |
| "The exfoliation comes from Maltobionic Acid — a PHA" | **False**: no PHA in the list [M: check file] |
| "Vitamin C derivative — boosts radiance" | **False**: none in the list; only kakadu plum extract |
| "Hyaluronic Acid — deep, lasting hydration at multiple skin layers" | **Overstated**: three forms at 5 ppm each, on the surface |
| "A peptide complex — firms skin and softens lines" | **Unproven** at these levels |
| "Niacinamide — fades dark spots and evens tone" | **Reasonable, if slow** (weeks to months) |
| "One Pad Replaces Your Whole Routine. Toner. Exfoliator. Serum. Treatment mask." | **No.** It replaces a toner and a sheet mask. You still need moisturiser and, by day, sunscreen |
| "Each pad works on your face, neck, chest, hands, elbows, knees, and underarms" | Harmless; it's a hydrating gel |
| "Hypoallergenic and dermatologist-tested" | No test published. The FDA says "hypoallergenic" has no legal meaning (from `01-mascara.md`) |
| "125.39% Improvement in Skin Hydration Immediately After Use (Temporary Effect)" and "100% agreed..." | From a test of the identical Anua pad on 21 women aged 20–59 (Dec 2024), measured right after first use [M: Anua image footnotes]. A short-lived effect. Not to be quoted on camera (section 11) |

---

## 3. How to use it, step by step

### 3.1 Before your first use: a patch test [R]

1. Take one pad. Wipe a small patch on the side of the jaw or behind the ear.
2. Leave it. Do this once a day for **2–3 days**.
3. If you get redness, itching, bumps or burning, don't use it.
4. **If you have a fish or soy allergy, don't patch-test: ask your doctor or allergist first** (section 11).

### 3.2 The everyday steps (the brand's "mask then wipe" method)

The brand's own steps are quoted in bold [M: image `tonnerfixed_6`]; the rest is plain-English detail [R].

| Step | What to do | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Wash your hands. | You'll be touching the face and the jar |
| 2 | Wash your face with a gentle cleanser and pat it almost dry with a clean towel. | **"After cleansing"** [M]. Slightly damp skin takes up water better (Seoul Beauty doctor: *"put your hydrating stuff on while your skin's still slightly damp"*) [M] |
| 3 | Open the jar and lift out **one** pad with the tweezers or a clean fingertip. Press it against the side of the jar to let extra gel drip back. | Keeps the jar clean; stops dripping |
| 4 | Close the lid at once. | Pads dry out if the lid is left open (Medicube: *"Keep the lid closed and sealed to prevent the pad from drying out"*) [M] |
| 5 | Find the slippery gel side. Lay it flat on one cheek, or tear/fold it to cover both cheeks or the forehead. | **"apply the 'Peptide Gel Side' to your skin"** [M] |
| 6 | Leave it **5 minutes**. Sit, brush your teeth, or dry your hair. | **"Leave it on for 5 minutes like a mini sheet mask"**; **"Use as a quick morning mask while drying your hair!"** [M] |
| 7 | Peel it off, turn it over, and **gently** sweep the fabric side across the whole face, from the centre outwards. Avoid the eyes and lips. | **"Use the 'Plain Side' to wipe along your skin texture"** [M]. Anua says *"excluding the area around your eyes and mouth"* [M: dodoskin] |
| 8 | Pat in what's left with your fingertips. Squeeze the pad onto your neck or the backs of your hands rather than waste it. | **"Lightly pat in the remaining essence"** [M] |
| 9 | Wait about a minute until it's no longer wet. | Moisturiser spreads better on tacky, not wet, skin [R] |
| 10 | Put on your moisturiser. In the morning, finish with sunscreen. | Seals the water in; protects from the sun (section 4) |

**How often:** the brand says **"Can be used every day, both AM & PM"** [M]. For dry, sensitive or older skin, once a day (night, or morning before makeup) is plenty to start [R].

### 3.3 The two other ways

- **Wipe only (fast, about 30 seconds):** skip step 6. Sweep the face with one side, then pat. Less hydration, quicker [R].
- **Mask only (calming):** lay the pad on dry or red patches for 5–10 minutes, then pat in; don't wipe [R].

### 3.4 Common beginner mistakes

| # | Mistake | Why it's a problem | Do this instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Scrubbing** with the pad | Thin mature skin tears and reddens easily. A beauty Short: *"rubbing too roughly can irritate your skin and cause even more redness"* [M: KuFEiyuZEdk] | Glide or press; never scrub |
| 2 | **Skipping moisturiser** because "one pad replaces your routine" | Humectants without a seal let water evaporate; skin can end up tighter (One Eye Beauty described a humectant serum that *"made it feel even more dry"*) [M: PEpiVhBo_g8] | Always follow with a moisturiser on dry skin |
| 3 | **Skipping sunscreen** and expecting spots to fade | Sun keeps making spots. Dr Dray: sunscreen *"will not only help in preventing more sunspots... but it also will help in fading existing"* ones [M: cmKERM23uhs] | Sunscreen every morning |
| 4 | **Leaving the lid open** | Pads dry out; the bottom of the jar goes dry | Close at once; store upside down for a while if the top pads dry (common tip [R]) |
| 5 | **Using fingers dipped straight in the jar** | Germs go in | Use the tweezers or wash hands |
| 6 | **Reusing a pad** the next day | Bacteria grow on wet fabric | One pad, one use |
| 7 | **Leaving it on for 20–30 minutes** "for more effect" | The gel dries and can start pulling water out of the skin as it does (as sheet masks can) [R] | 5–10 minutes max |
| 8 | **Wiping over the eyes and lips** | Stings and wastes product | Go around them |
| 9 | **Stacking it with acid pads, scrubs and retinol on the same night** | Too much at once irritates mature skin | This pad is gentle, but keep other strong steps on separate nights at first |
| 10 | **Expecting a facelift** | Skincare *"can only do so much"* (Dr Idriss) [M] | Expect plumper, dewier, calmer skin |
| 11 | **Judging it after one week for spots** | Niacinamide studies ran 4–12 weeks [M] | Take a photo on day 1 and at week 8 in the same light |
| 12 | **Calling a breakout "purging"** | Purging only happens with acids or retinoids; this pad has neither [R] | If you break out, stop and patch test |
| 13 | **Using it if you're allergic to fish** | Salmon DNA, collagen of unknown source | Ask a doctor first |

### 3.5 How long before you see anything [R, based on the studies above]

| When | What to expect | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Minutes** | Softer, plumper, dewier, smoother makeup | Water in the top layer; the gel film. Anua's test measured a big hydration jump *right after* use [M] |
| **Hours** | The glow fades by evening | Hydration is temporary. Doctorly: *"It's not a permanent effect, but it's an immediate effect"* [M] |
| **2–4 weeks** of daily use | Skin may feel less tight and rough; makeup flakes less | Consistent hydration and a better-supported barrier (niacinamide helps skin make ceramides) |
| **4–12 weeks** | Possibly slightly more even tone, softer-looking fine lines | Niacinamide trials: 4 weeks for spots (Hakozaki), 12 weeks for lines and spots (Bissett). Unknown niacinamide level here |
| **Never** | Lifted jowls, firmer neck, less sagging, collagen rebuilt | Those come from bone, fat and deep collagen; pads don't reach them |

---

## 4. Where it fits in a routine

### 4.1 The order, morning and night

| Step | Morning | Night |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rinse with water or a gentle cleanser | **Remove makeup** (micellar water, a water-like cleanser you wipe on with cotton and don't rinse off; or a cleansing oil or balm), then a **gentle cleanser** |
| 2 | **Luxe pad** (mask 5 min, wipe, pat) | **Luxe pad** |
| 3 | Optional serum (vitamin C or other) | Optional treatment: **retinoid** (retinol or prescription tretinoin) on some nights |
| 4 | Eye cream or balm (optional) | Eye cream or balm (optional) |
| 5 | **Moisturiser** | **Moisturiser** (richer at night) |
| 6 | **Sunscreen SPF 30+**, then makeup | — |

[R, following the cleanse–treat–protect order in section 1.3.]

### 4.2 What it goes well with [R]

| Pairs well with | Why |
|---|---|
| A **moisturiser with ceramides** or a richer cream | Seals in the water the pad adds; ceramides refill the barrier (Doctorly recommend ceramide moisturisers for mature skin [M]) |
| **Sunscreen** | Protects; needed for any hope of spots fading |
| **Retinol / tretinoin** | The pad has no acids, so it adds no extra sting. Using a hydrating step under or before a retinoid is a common way to make retinoids easier to tolerate |
| **Vitamin C serum** | Fine together. The old warning that niacinamide and vitamin C "cancel out" comes from outdated lab conditions [R] |
| **Peptide serum** | Fine; both gentle |
| **Makeup** | Gel pads give a dewy base. Wait a minute so it's not wet |

### 4.3 What not to mix it with, or be careful with [R]

| Be careful with | Why |
|---|---|
| **Another toner pad or acid toner** on the same night | Doubling up is pointless and can over-wipe thin skin |
| **Scrubs** and cleansing brushes on the same day | Adds rubbing on top of rubbing |
| **Prescription creams for eczema or rosacea** (eczema: itchy, dry, sore patches; rosacea: long-lasting redness and flushing, often on the cheeks and nose) | Ask the prescriber; extra layers can change how they work |
| **Very strong acid peels or a fresh in-clinic treatment** (laser: a beam of light that heats the skin; peel: a strong acid that makes the top layer flake off; microneedling: tiny needles that make small holes so the skin repairs itself) | Follow the clinic's aftercare; use nothing new for a few days |

There is **no dangerous combination** with this pad. It's a gentle hydrating gel [R].

### 4.4 Simple example routines

**A. The absolute beginner (3 products) [R]**

| Morning | Night |
|---|---|
| Rinse → Luxe pad (wipe only) → moisturiser with SPF 30+ | Gentle cleanser → Luxe pad (5-min mask) → moisturiser |

**B. A woman over 60 with dry, thin, easily irritated skin [R]**
(Built from what dermatologists in the sources recommend for menopausal skin: gentle cleansing once a day, hydration, a rich moisturiser, retinoid if tolerated, sunscreen daily [M: Dr Idriss, Dr Dray, Dr Sam Ellis, Doctorly].)

| Morning | Night |
|---|---|
| 1. Splash with lukewarm water only (Dr Idriss: *"cleanse once a day keep it to the night time"*) [M] | 1. Remove makeup with a cleansing milk or balm |
| 2. **Luxe pad as a 5-minute mask** while you do your hair; then press in, don't wipe | 2. Hydrating cream cleanser |
| 3. Moisturiser for dry skin | 3. **Luxe pad**, gentle wipe, pat |
| 4. Sunscreen SPF 30+ (a hydrating one) on face, neck and backs of hands | 4. Two or three nights a week: a gentle retinol (pea-size), building up slowly; skip if it stings |
| 5. Makeup, if worn | 5. Rich ceramide night cream, also on the neck |
| | 6. Squeeze the leftover pad gel onto the backs of the hands, then hand cream |

**C. Sensitive or rosacea-prone skin [R]:** cleanser → pad (mask only, no wiping) → plain fragrance-free moisturiser → sunscreen in the morning. Patch test first; stop if it flushes.

**D. Oily or combination skin over 50 [R]:** pad in the morning only, a lighter gel moisturiser, sunscreen. At night, a retinoid or an exfoliant does more for pores than this pad.

**E. Budget version [R]:** the pad is a "nice to have". A gentle cleanser, a plain moisturiser and sunscreen do most of the work. Anua's identical pad costs less per pad (section 7).

---

## 5. Who it's for, and who it isn't

### 5.1 By skin type, age, sensitivity and budget [R]

| | Good fit | Poor fit |
|---|---|---|
| **Skin type** | Dry; dehydrated (tight, dull); normal; combination with dry cheeks | Very oily; acne-prone (it doesn't clear pores; BestReviews says the Anua pad is *"Not the best option for more oily skin types"* [M]) |
| **Sensitivity** | People who can't tolerate retinol, acids or fragrance | Fish-, soy- or corn-allergic people; very reactive rosacea (patch test) |
| **Age** | 40s to 80s with dryness and dullness; also younger dry skin | Anyone hoping for lifting or firming |
| **Goal** | Hydration, glow, a calm makeup base, a quick "mask" habit | Exfoliating rough texture, fading deep spots fast, shrinking pores |
| **Lifestyle** | People who want a quick, no-mess step | People who dislike waste or bulky jars |
| **Budget** | Those happy to pay about $0.43 a use for convenience | Tight budgets: a plain hydrating toner on cotton does a similar job for less [R]; the Anua version is cheaper per pad |
| **Values** | — | Vegans: salmon DNA is an animal ingredient |
| **Eye area** | Pat the leftover gel gently under the eyes | Not for the eyelids or the lash line: wipe around the eyes, not over them (Anua says to avoid the eye area [M: dodoskin]) |

### 5.2 How skin changes after 50 and 60, and why

**The big driver is menopause.** Menopause officially starts 12 months after the last period; the skin changes start years earlier in perimenopause (the years of change before periods stop) [M: Dr Sam Ellis, N7SgoUm_YDE]. Estrogen, which helps keep skin thick, plump, oily and quick to heal, falls [M: Dr Sam Ellis; Dr Dray cmKERM23uhs].

| Change | What happens, and why | What it looks and feels like | Can this pad help? [R] |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Collagen loss** | *"nearly a third lost in the first 5 years and a decline of 2.1% per year over the 15 years following menopause"* [M: Kamp et al., *Clin Exp Dermatol* 2022;47(12):2117]. Dr Dray: *"women lose about 30 percent of collagen in the first five years after menopause"* [M]. (The 30% figure comes from older, small studies measured on the forearm [M: search result note]) | Thinner skin, crepey patches, fine lines that deepen | **No** for collagen; **yes** for the look of fine lines for a few hours (plumping) |
| **Dryness** | The same review paper links menopause to dry, itchy skin: *"Pruritus is also frequently seen in postmenopausal women, commonly with associated xerosis"* (pruritus = itching; xerosis = dry skin), and says that *"the hypo-oestrogenic state could be implicated in increased TEWL"* (in plain words: low oestrogen may be why more water escapes through the skin) [M: Kamp et al. 2022, Part 2, PMC10092853, page loaded 2026-10-31]. Skin holds water less well: *"the skin loses some of its ability to hold on to water"* [M: Dr Dray]. Dr Dray: dry skin is *"probably the most striking change that people notice early on"* [M] | Tight after washing, flaky patches, makeup clings to dry bits, itching | **Yes, the pad's main strength**, together with a moisturiser |
| **A weaker barrier and more sensitivity** | Ceramides decline: *"ceramides which are fats found in our Skin Barrier the levels of those start to decline with age making you more prone to water loss dryness and irritation"* [M: Dr Dray]. Hormonal flushing, rosacea flares [M: Dr Idriss] | Old products suddenly sting; redness; "my skin hates everything" | **Partly**: fragrance-free, acid-free, with niacinamide. Still patch test |
| **Dullness** | Cell turnover slows, so dead cells pile up and reflect light unevenly [M: Doctorly 1NOgLh46HdM: *"a decrease in cell turnover, which can make your skin look more dull"*] | Grey, tired, "lost my glow" | **Yes, short term** (water + the gentle wipe). An exfoliant does more long term |
| **Fine lines and wrinkles** | Less collagen and elastin; sun damage adds up | Lines around eyes, mouth, forehead | **A little, for hours** (plumping); niacinamide and adenosine may help slightly over weeks |
| **Thinning and crepey skin** | Less collagen and estrogen; Dr Sam Ellis: *"the skin becomes thin and crepy"* [M] | Neck, chest, arms, eyelids look like crepe paper | **No real change**; hydration softens the look for a while |
| **Age spots (sun spots)** | Years of sun; and *"your skin becomes more susceptible to UV damage as you go through menopause"* [M: Dr Sam Ellis]. Dr Idriss: after a sunny week *"you probably look more spotted"* [M] | Brown spots on cheeks, hands, chest | **Slowly, maybe** (niacinamide, over months) **only with daily sunscreen**. New or changing spots should be checked by a doctor [M: Dr Dray] |
| **Sagging, jowls, hollows** | Bone shrinks and fat pads move: *"your eye sockets are widening... your Jawbone is receding"* [M: Dr Sam Ellis]; *"skin care products they don't tackle this"* [M: Dr Dray] | Jowls, droopy cheeks, hollow eyes, "my face fell" | **No** |
| **Slower healing, easy bruising** | Estrogen helps healing; thinner skin bruises [M: Dr Dray] | Cuts linger; bruises on hands and shins | No; but **gentle handling** (press, don't scrub) matters more now |
| **New facial hair, adult acne** | Relatively more male hormones [M: Dr Dray] | Chin hairs; jawline spots | No |

**After 60 and 70 [R, extending the sources]:** everything above continues more slowly; skin is drier and thinner still, so gentle **pressing instead of wiping** and a richer moisturiser on top become more important. The 5-minute mask is the most useful way to use the pad at this age. Many women want fewer steps: *"I want one or two items for an easy routine. Don't want to do 15 products for my 60 year old skin."* (@lorimogan4983, 120 likes, Doctorly 1NOgLh46HdM) [M].

**"Age is not a skin type."** Dr Idriss: *"the first thing you need to do when you're building a skin care routine is to base it off of your problem"* [M]. A viewer: *"'Age is not a skin type' hope this phrase catches on"* (@jacqchurchm2293, 177 likes) [M]. In practice [R]: an oily 60-year-old and a dry 60-year-old need different things; this pad suits the dry one.

---

## 6. Every problem the viewer has, one by one

Quotes are word for word, with source and likes. Where a quote is about a different product, it says so.

### 6.1 Dry, tight skin

- **What it is:** skin that feels tight after washing, looks flaky, and drinks up moisturiser.
- **Why it happens:** less oil and less water-holding after menopause; a weaker barrier (section 5.2).
- **How it feels:** *"You spend your whole life learning how to take care of your skin; developing a routine, and suddenly - menopause - and you don't know your skin or body anymore."* (@charmainerohde8575, **3.6K likes**, Doctorly 1NOgLh46HdM) [M]. A dermatologist describes her patients: *"Their skin is dry, it's flaky, and they'll tell me every always, 'This hasn't been a problem for me before'"* (Doctorly) [M].
- **What they've tried, and why it failed [R]:** foaming cleansers that strip more oil; heavier and heavier creams on top of dehydrated skin; drinking more water (*"it's a little overhyped"*, Seoul Beauty doctor [M]).
- **How the pad helps:** a dose of water and humectants straight after washing, then sealed in with cream. On PDRN skincare: *"My skin is super dry, and PDRN really helps keep it moisturized without any irritation."* (@chojiae-f2e, 50 likes, Dr Dray video) [M]. **Doesn't help** if used without a moisturiser on very dry skin.

### 6.2 Dull, "tired", grey skin

- **What:** skin that has lost its glow.
- **Why:** slower cell turnover, dryness and rougher surface scatter light (section 1.6).
- **How it feels:** *"I didn't realize a lack of estrogen was contributing to my hollowness and jowling and not just dry skin!"* (@ITFDBLA, 130 likes, Dr Idriss) [M].
- **Tried and failed [R]:** more makeup (sits in dry patches); harsh scrubs (tiny scratches; *"they actually leave these tiny scratches"*, Seoul Beauty doctor [M]).
- **How the pad helps:** instant dewiness for hours. **Doesn't** remove dead-cell build-up the way an exfoliant does.

### 6.3 Fine lines and crepey texture

- **What:** small lines and crinkled, thin patches.
- **Why:** collagen and elastin loss plus sun damage.
- **How it feels:** *"I have really dry skin under my eyes that make my lines appear worse than they could be."* (@michellelindensmith7033, 56, 20 likes, Doctorly) [M]. *"My face is reaping the benefits but my neck can't always handle all of the skincare."* (@JillJ, 741 likes) [M].
- **Tried and failed:** collagen creams (collagen *"is not going to penetrate your skin"*, Doctorly [M]); retinol that stung or peeled.
- **How the pad helps:** dehydration lines plump up for hours. Real collagen change needs retinoids or procedures [M: Doctorly, Dr Sam Ellis]. **Doesn't firm.**

### 6.4 Dark spots and uneven tone

- **What:** brown sun or age spots; blotchy tone.
- **Why:** years of sun; skin more UV-sensitive after menopause.
- **How it feels:** *"As a 54yo female who spent her teens & 20's BAKING in the sun, I appreciate a Derm who speaks the TRUTH!"* (@i_am_whole_again, 478 likes, Dr Idriss) [M]. *"we went to the beach with baby oil on our skin"* (@explorewithsteggie1913, 108 likes) [M].
- **Tried and failed [R]:** vitamin C serums that irritated; strong peels; concealer.
- **How the pad helps:** niacinamide can lighten spots a little over 4–12 weeks (studies at 5%) **if** sunscreen is worn daily. PDRN's brightening is unproven. Expect "a bit more even", not "faded".

### 6.5 Sensitive skin that reacts to "actives"

- **What:** skin that stings, burns or reddens with retinol, acids, vitamin C or fragrance.
- **Why:** weaker barrier with age (fewer ceramides).
- **How it feels:** *"I don't think this works well on sensitive skin. I wish it did, but it created more sensitivity for me."* (@rupaj9094, on an exfoliating toner pad, EmpVHOGyYLw) [M]. From the earlier round: *"the one thing that i so relieved is a VEGAN, because my skin is very sensitive."* (@skinloverishere, 57 likes) [M].
- **Tried and failed:** acid toners, retinol, perfumed creams.
- **How the pad helps:** no acids, no retinol, no fragrance, no alcohol. **Watch:** niacinamide and plant extracts can still sting very reactive skin. Patch test.

### 6.6 Rough texture and bumps

- **What:** sandpaper feel, flaky bits, tiny bumps.
- **Why:** dead-cell build-up, dryness.
- **How it feels:** *"during seasonal changes, my cheeks become dry. My cheeks get rough, and my skin deteriorates as if my immune system is weakening"* (Ulta, most-helpful positive review of the Mediheal Collagen Ampoule Pad, review R-069) [M]. *"I struggle with texture on my higher cheek bones and on my chin, under my bottom lip"* (Aly, Dallas, Ulta, 2026-10-22, 5 stars, on Medicube's exfoliating Zero Pore Pad, review C-029) [M].
- **What they tried, and why it failed:**
  1. **Accepting it.** The same Mediheal reviewer: *"Even dermatologists say it's just due to seasonal changes and stress, so I've accepted that I can't fix it and am destined to use only gentle skincare products"* (R-069) [M].
  2. **Scrubs.** They feel smoothing but can scratch: *"they actually leave these tiny scratches"* (Seoul Beauty doctor, 864b16vfJQk) [M].
  3. **Strong exfoliating pads.** They can backfire: *"after four days, I began to see new breakouts. I lessened the use to three times a week, until I was only using it once a week. ... Even once a week, I was getting small irritated red bumps"* (Ulta, most-helpful critical review of the Medicube Zero Pore Pad, review C-032) [M]. Why [R]: thin, dry mature skin often can't take acid or rubbing every day.
- **How the pad helps [R]:** softens through hydration and a gentle fabric wipe. **It does not exfoliate chemically**, despite the page. An acid (lactic or mandelic for sensitive skin, per Dr Idriss and Doctorly [M]) once or twice a week does more.

### 6.7 Makeup that looks cakey or clings to dry patches

- **Why:** foundation sticks to flakes and sinks into lines on dry skin [R].
- **What they tried, and why it failed:**
  1. **Putting makeup straight on top of a wet pad.** It can flake or "pill" (roll into little crumbs): *"These pads always make my skin peel and also peel under makeup"* (Sephora Middle East, 2026-08-26, 1 star, Anua pad, review A-025) [M]; *"I wanted to love this but I could not use it with anything without experiencing pilling"* (Lookfantastic, 2026-06-04, 2 stars, Anua pad, review A-022) [M]. Why [R]: the gel is still wet and tacky, so the next layer rubs it into crumbs.
  2. **Rubbing in a lot of extra liquid, then cream.** *"I didn’t like how my skin felt after I first used these and rubbed everything in- I felt sticky or tacky ... I wound up having to wipe my face with a damp cloth to remove the ballies (pilling)"* (glowrecipe.com, 2026-02-19, 3 stars, Glow Recipe PDRN pads, review R-005) [M].
  3. **What worked for others:** waiting. *"the finish is quite rich and tacky, so give it a few minutes to sink in before applying makeup to avoid pilling"* (Vitacost, 2026-06-13, 5 stars, Anua pad, review A-036) [M].
- **How the pad helps:** a 5-minute mask before makeup gives a smoother, dewier base. Anua buyer: *"It hydrates my skin, calms redness and it's pefect under makeup"* (Lucia, Cult Beauty, 2026-04-14, 5 stars, review A-012) [M]. Commenters ask: *"Do you have any recommendations on what toner pads to use before makeup?"* (@laradevlin4503) [M].

### 6.8 Too many steps, too confusing

- **What:** overwhelm.
- **How it feels:** *"I found this all so confusing. I think I better watch it for the third time now."* (@tydjjdyt8561, Doctorly) [M]. *"I want one or two items for an easy routine."* (@lorimogan4983, 120 likes) [M].
- **How the pad helps:** one jar does the toner and mask steps; nothing to measure. **But** it does not replace moisturiser or sunscreen.

### 6.9 Wasted money and distrust of hype

- **How it feels:** *"Products like skin medica are an absolute fortune and sure don't have the results to go with the price tag."* (@lorae7, Dr Idriss) [M]. *"OK I think you need to do a followup of drugstore products for over 50s because this stuff is PRICEY."* (@tw3894, 42 likes) [M]. On PDRN: *"I regret not checking dr dray and wasting my money lol"* (@wiist_eria) [M]; *"takes PDRN serum out the cart"* (@usamakhan3553, 153 likes) [M]; *"This hype is huge in S Korea now. I'm living here and I can tell it's all marketing, hype and trends."* (@marinatorres3473, 82 likes) [M].
- **How the pad fits [R]:** a premium price for a hydrating pad. Honest framing is the antidote: "a nice hydrating mask, not a miracle".

### 6.10 "It looks the same" (no visible change)

- **How it feels:** on a Medicube PDRN gel pad demo (846,000 views): *"Looks the same to me"* (@Mimi0427-c6h, **921 likes**); *"Is the calmer skin in the room with us????"* (@iloveLmorethanyou) [M].
- **Why:** hydration changes are subtle on camera; the "repair" claims don't show in minutes [R].
- **What they tried, and why it failed:**
  1. **One PDRN product after another.** *"In K-beauty right now, PDRN is everywhere. I've tried a lot of them hoping for good results, but honestly ... Most products didn't really live up to the hype"* (@zz9zz6, 168 likes, Dr Dray video XC2OZDLpEJ0, review C-053; the one that worked for this person was VT's) [M].
  2. **Using a whole bottle.** *"I used an entire bottle of the pdrn. While it felt good, I'm uncertain whether it actually did anything"* (@zoeberry33, on Anua's PDRN serum, review C-105) [M].
  3. **Pads used daily for weeks.** *"They do seem to moisturize in the short term but haven't noticed any change in my skin. I guess nothing is magic."* (RAH, Atlanta, Ulta, 2026-09-08, 4 stars, Mediheal Collagen Ampoule Pad, review R-067) [M]. After 3 weeks of Biodance's collagen gel pads morning and night: *"I'm not seeing firmness or elasticity"* (YouTube creator, bl-nELE-670, review R-134) [M].
  4. **The injections themselves.** *"once the swelling went down my under eyes looked identical to how they did before"* (@Criticalbeauty411, after Rejuran injections, review C-097) [M].
  - Why it failed [R]: what these products really do is add water, which shows as softness and glow for some hours, then fades. People were hoping for firming, which they can't give.
- **What this means:** set expectations: feel and dewiness, not a new face.

### 6.11 Pads drying out, jar too big, two sides confusing

- *"Do they dry out quickly?"* (@Mirendawali, 11 likes) [M]. *"the packaging takes up a lot of space"*; two-sided pad confusing (Anua buyers, Cult Beauty) [M]. *"I'm completely flummoxed by the two sided pad which I can't perceive, being completely saturated with serum"* (Qwendy, Cult Beauty, 2026-05-05, 4 stars, Anua pad, review A-008) [M].
- **What they tried, and why it failed:**
  1. **Travelling with the jar.** *"every time I go out of town and pack my facial products, almost always these pads find a way to tussle and turn about in my luggage and somehow open and leak"* (Lei, Ulta, 2026-07-20, 4 stars, Mediheal Watermide pad, review R-078) [M]. An Anua buyer: *"if you hold it at an angle for even a second, serum will leak out of the box"* (Ulta, most-helpful critical review, review A-060) [M]. Why [R]: the jar is full of loose liquid, and the lid is not a tight seal.
  2. **Following the box instructions.** *"the instructions for use are confusing as they say to leave on skin, but I'm assuming that's for targeted areas. I swipe the pad all over my face"* (Mhairi, Lookfantastic, 2026-07-13, 5 stars, Anua pad, review A-017) [M].
  3. **Wiping with a slippery gel pad.** *"they are very slippery and normally when I wipe them across my face like it will slip out. So I found the best way is to really hold it with your thumb and all of your fingers"* (YouTube creator, Biodance gel pads, review R-134) [M].
  4. **The tweezers.** *"the included tweezers are too blunt, often picking up two pads at once"* (Ulta, Mediheal Watermide pad, review R-084) [M].
- **Fix [R]:** close the lid; keep it upright, or flip it for a day so the top pads get wet again; use the slippery side to mask; hold the pad flat with all your fingers to wipe; travel with the jar in a sealed bag.

### 6.12 Sagging and jowls

- **How it feels (from the earlier audience round):** *"When I turned 48, my face just fell."* [M: `SUMMARY.md` section 4].
- **Why:** bone loss and fat shifting.
- **How the pad helps:** **it doesn't.** Dr Dray: *"skin care products they don't tackle this"* [M].

### 6.13 Fish allergy, vegan, "salmon sperm" disgust

- **What:** worry or squeamishness about fish ingredients.
- **How it feels:** *"I want to know more about the different types of PDRN- salmon, rose, aloe etc."* (@Leighalinkous) [M]; *"Totally thought the title was 'PORN Skincare'"* (@harmony323, 390 likes) [M].
- **How the pad fits:** it **is** salmon-derived, and not vegan (section 11).

### 7.2 When to pick this product, and when something else [R]

| Pick the Luxe pad if... | Pick something else if... |
|---|---|
| You want a big jar (140 pads) of gentle, fragrance-free, acid-free hydrating gel pads | You want the same thing cheaper per pad → **Anua** (identical ingredients) |
| You can't tolerate retinol, acids or fragrance | You want smoother texture or clearer pores → an **exfoliating pad** (Biodance, Rejuran) or a lactic-acid product |
| You like the 5-minute mini-mask habit | You want the most PDRN in a pad → **Biodance** (1,000 ppm) |
| Your skin is dry and dull and you want glow before makeup | You're vegan or fish-allergic → a **plant-PDRN** or non-PDRN pad (VT, Isntree, Torriden) |
| | You want proven anti-ageing → a **retinoid** at night and **sunscreen** by day; pads are extras |
| | You dislike waste → a **bottle toner** or a facial spray |

## 8. Questions viewers ask, with plain answers

Sources for the questions: comments on the videos in section 12 (marked with the commenter where the wording is theirs) and the search titles that perform ("When Should You Use Toner Pads?", "2 ways to use toner pads", "Which Korean Toner Pads Suit Your Skin?") [M]. Answers are [R] unless a source is named.

| # | Question | Plain answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **What is PDRN, actually?** | Tiny pieces of DNA, usually from salmon. On the label it's "Sodium DNA" |
| 2 | **Is this the same as the "salmon sperm facial" Kim Kardashian had?** | Same family of ingredient, very different thing. The facial injects it under the skin; the pad leaves a tiny amount (about 0.01%) on top |
| 3 | **Does PDRN in a pad get into my skin?** | Very little, if any. The pieces are far too big to pass through intact skin (Dermatology Times, 2026) [M] |
| 4 | **So does it work?** | As a moisturising, glow-giving pad, yes. As "cellular repair", there's no good evidence. Dr Dray: "doubtful that these skincare products with PDRN do anything above and beyond moisturize the skin" [M] |
| 5 | **Is it the same as the Anua pad?** | Same 45 ingredients, same design [M]. Anua costs less per pad |
| 6 | **Which side do I use first?** | The slippery gel side as a 5-minute mask, then the fabric side to wipe |
| 7 | **Do I have to leave it on 5 minutes?** | No. Wipe only if you're in a hurry; the mask gives more hydration |
| 8 | **Do I rinse it off?** | No. Pat in the leftover gel |
| 9 | **Do I need a moisturiser after?** | Yes, especially if your skin is dry. The pad adds water; a cream keeps it in |
| 10 | **Can I use it before makeup?** (@laradevlin4503) | Yes. Wait a minute until it's not wet |
| 11 | **Morning or night?** | Either or both. Start with once a day |
| 12 | **Can I use it with retinol?** (@habsign4355: "I use retinal but I'm scared to add a toner pad") | Yes. This pad has no acids or retinol, so it doesn't add sting. Use the pad first, let it dry, then the retinol, then cream |
| 13 | **Can I use it with vitamin C?** | Yes |
| 14 | **Can I use it with my acid toner / peel pads?** | Better on different nights so you don't over-wipe |
| 15 | **Is it good for dry mature skin?** (@AmtulAfiyaa: "For Dry skin?") | Yes, that's who it suits best |
| 16 | **Is it good for oily mature skin?** (@momkashi) | It's fine, but it won't control oil or pores |
| 17 | **Will it fade my age spots?** | Maybe a little, slowly, thanks to niacinamide, and only with daily sunscreen. Don't expect them gone |
| 18 | **Will it firm my neck or lift my jowls?** | No |
| 19 | **Will it help wrinkles?** | Fine lines look softer for a few hours. Deep wrinkles: no |
| 20 | **Does it exfoliate?** | Only the gentle rub of the fabric. There are no exfoliating acids in it, whatever the page says |
| 21 | **I broke out. Is it purging?** | No. Purging only comes from acids or retinoids. Stop, and patch test |
| 22 | **Is it safe if I'm allergic to fish?** | Don't use it without asking your doctor or allergist. It contains salmon DNA, and the collagen source isn't stated |
| 23 | **Is it vegan?** (@sarahnir8895) | No. It's salmon-derived. (The pad *fabric* is called "vegan fabric"; the liquid isn't) |
| 24 | **What's the difference between salmon, rose and aloe PDRN?** (@Leighalinkous) | Salmon PDRN comes from fish and has the injection research behind it. Plant "PDRN" (ginseng, rose, carrot, seaweed) is plant DNA with no clinical data on skin (Dr Dray) [M] |
| 25 | **Do the pads dry out?** (@Mirendawali) | They can, near the bottom. Close the lid tightly every time |
| 26 | **How long does a jar last?** | 70 days at twice a day; 140 days at once a day (140 pads) |
| 27 | **Can I use it on my neck, chest and hands?** | Yes; squeeze the leftover gel there |
| 28 | **Can I use it under my eyes?** | Pat the leftover gel gently below the eye; don't wipe over the eyelids or lash line (Anua says avoid the eye area) [M] |
| 29 | **Does it have fragrance?** (@pamelasault9483 asked about fragrance types) | None listed |
| 30 | **Can I use it with rosacea or eczema?** | Possibly, as a mask without wiping, after a patch test. Ask your dermatologist if you're on prescription creams |
| 31 | **Can I cut a pad in half?** | Yes; it's just fabric and gel. Use both halves at once |
| 32 | **Should I keep it in the fridge?** | Not needed. A cool pad feels nice, though; keep the lid shut |
| 33 | **Is it safe in pregnancy?** | No pregnancy data for this product. Lab-made growth-factor peptides (oligopeptide-1) have insufficient pregnancy safety data [M: search summary]. Ask your doctor |
| 34 | **Aren't toner pads bad for the environment?** | Each pad is single-use fabric. A dermatologist Short with 3.6M views says so [M]. A bottle toner or a mist creates less waste |
| 35 | **Is it worth it?** (Short jxM3Vtfz4Wc asks exactly this) | Worth it if you'll use the mask effect and like the convenience; otherwise a bottle toner does the wiping job for less |

## 9. Myths and truths

| Myth | Truth | Source |
|---|---|---|
| "It's the same as the salmon-sperm injection" | The injection puts PN/PDRN into the dermis; the pad leaves about 0.01% on the surface | Dr Dray; Dermatology Times; Anua 100 ppm [M] |
| "99% PDRN" means the pad is 99% PDRN | It's the raw material's purity. Pads hold 0.01 to 1,000 ppm | One Eye Beauty; retailer INCIs [M] |
| "PDRN repairs skin at the cellular level" | Shown for injections into wounds, and in lab dishes; not shown for creams or pads | Dr Dray; Dermatology Times [M] |
| "Collagen in the pad builds my collagen" | Collagen can't soak in as collagen. It's a moisturiser | Doctorly [M] |
| "The hyaluronic acid gives deep, lasting hydration at multiple skin layers" (Luxe page) | The pad has 5 ppm of each HA form, working on the surface; the level studied in a wrinkle trial was 0.1% (200 times more) | Anua ppm; Pavicic 2011 [M] |
| "Plant PDRN is the same as salmon PDRN" | Different source and size; no clinical data on skin | Dr Dray [M] |
| "One pad replaces your whole routine" | It replaces a toner and a sheet mask. You still need moisturiser and sunscreen | [R] |
| "Toner balances your pH, so you must use one" | That was true with old soaps; modern cleansers are pH-balanced. Today a toner is optional | Dermatologist Short jo9nvX8NkK4; Doctorly [M] |
| "More steps = better skin" | Doctors say pick products for your problem; the 10-step routine is optional | Seoul Beauty doctor; Doctorly [M] |
| "A breakout means it's purging" | No acids or retinoids, so no purging | [R] |
| "Glass skin is permanent" | The dewy look from hydration is immediate but temporary | Doctorly [M] |
| "Skincare can lift sagging skin" | Sagging comes from bone and fat changes | Dr Dray; Dr Idriss; Dr Sam Ellis [M] |
| "Natural/salmon means safe for everyone" | Fish- and soy-allergic people need care | INCI [M] |
| "If it's expensive and clinical-looking it works" | The same formula sells for less as Anua | Prices [M] |
| "It's too late to protect my skin at 60" | Dr Sam Ellis: *"it is never too little or too late"*; sunscreen helps at any age | [M] |

## 11. Safety, and what you can and can't say on camera

**Safety [M: INCI; R: advice]**
- **Fish allergy:** contains salmon DNA; the collagen source isn't stated. BestReviews: *"People with fish or seafood allergies should avoid salmon-derived PDRN pads."* [M]. Experts disagree on whether purification removes fish proteins; no study was found [M: search]. Say: "If you're allergic to fish, ask your doctor first."
- **Soy and corn:** soybean ferment, possibly soy lecithin; corn extract.
- **Not vegan.**
- **Patch test** 2–3 days; stop if it stings, burns, itches or causes spots.
- **Keep out of the eyes and off the lips.** Press, don't scrub, on thin skin.
- **New or changing spots** should be seen by a doctor, not treated with pads [M: Dr Dray].

**What you can't say (drug-type or unsupported claims) [R, per `SUMMARY.md` section 5]**
- Can't say: "repairs skin at the cellular level", "regenerates", "rebuilds collagen", "boosts collagen", "lifts", "firms", "fades dark spots in 4 weeks", "clinically proven", "the same as Korean clinic injections", "exfoliates with PHA", "vitamin C", "99% PDRN", "vegan", "hypoallergenic", "zero side effects", "125% more hydration" or "100% saw results" (those are Anua's test numbers).
- Can say: "a gentle, fragrance-free, acid-free hydrating gel pad"; "skin looks plumper and dewier right after"; "use it as a 5-minute mini mask"; "contains niacinamide, which can help skin tone look more even over weeks"; "contains salmon-derived PDRN; the science on PDRN in skincare is still early".

# Part 2: The reviews

*Reviews: Luxe PDRN Toner Pads (product 2), plus the same formula, rivals and toner pads in general*

Pulled 2026-10-31 by a review-collecting helper, following `packs/PACK-BRIEF.md` ("The reviews"). A second pull later the same day (a finishing helper, after the check in `checks/packs/02-pdrn-toner-pads-pack-check.md`) added 52 more reviews and comments: R-111 to R-136 and C-085 to C-110. Raw pulls: `data/reviews/toner-pads/` (start with `sources-log-2026-10-31.md`); the second pull is in `data/reviews/toner-pads/round3-2026-10-31/`. Earlier report reused: `02-pdrn-toner-pads.md` and `data/toner-pads/youtube-pulls-2026-10-31.md`.

**How to read this.** Every review is copied word for word (typos kept), with where it came from, the date, the stars or likes, and the reviewer's age or skin type when they gave it. **[M]** = measured (read off a page or file on 2026-10-31). **[R]** = my reading. Counts of themes are my reading [R]: each review was sorted by word-matching and then checked by eye, so treat them as close, not exact. YouTube dates are relative ("1 year ago") and are shown that way. The pages were loaded normally, once each; Amazon and Reddit were not loaded (bot checks), and no site's bot check was worked around, so each store gives only the reviews on its first page.

**Labels.** Every review carries the exact product. The ID letter says how close it is to the Luxe pads:

| Letter | Meaning | Reviews |
|---|---|---|
| L | The Luxe PDRN Toner Pads themselves | 7 |
| A | Anua PDRN 100 Hyaluronic Acid Glow Pad: the same ingredient list as the Luxe pads (see `02-pdrn-toner-pads.md` section 1.4) | 73 |
| R | Close rivals: other PDRN pads and hydrating gel or collagen pads (Medicube, Mediheal, VT, Biodance, Glow Recipe, Rejuran, Isntree, SNP, numbuzin) | 136 |
| C | The category: other toner pads (mostly exfoliating ones), PDRN skincare in general (serums, creams, injections) and Korean skincare in general | 110 |
| | **Total** | **326** (319 not counting the Luxe page's own testimonials) |

## 1. What the reviews tell us, in plain English

1. **No real customer reviews of the Luxe pads were found anywhere.** The only Luxe reviews are 7 testimonials printed on Luxe's own product page (section 4). They give ages from 42 to 63 and talk about dark spots and crepey neck skin. These are testimonials the brand prints on its own page, not independent reviews, so they are kept apart from the counts. Amazon could not be loaded. [M]
2. **The Anua pad, which has the same recipe, is well liked.** I read 73 Anua reviews. Of the 63 with stars: 48 gave 5 stars, 9 gave 4, 2 gave 3, 2 gave 2 and 2 gave 1. [M] The stores' own averages: Ulta 4.8 from 124 reviews, iHerb 4.7 from 127, Lookfantastic 4.68 from 44, Cult Beauty 4.67 from 18, Vitacost 4.6 from 20, Sephora Middle East 4.11 from 19, anua.us 4.9 from 15 [M, 2026-10-31]. The Anua pad has two prices, both measured 2026-10-31: **$23.00 for 60 pads on Anua's own US store** (anua.us product data, about $0.38 a pad) and **$22.00 for 60 at Ulta and Vitacost** (about $0.37 a pad) [M]. Luxe sells its version for $59.95 for 140 pads (about $0.43 a pad) [M, luxeresearchlab.com product data].
3. **What people love is the water.** 38 of the 73 Anua reviews say "hydrating" or "moisturising", and 25 talk about glow (a fresh, shiny, healthy look). [M, word count] The pads come soaking wet, feel cool, and leave skin soft and plump straight away. Many say the effect is instant: "Right after, my skin felt hydrated, looked glowy".
4. **Most people use them as a mini face mask, not a wipe.** 19 Anua reviews describe leaving a pad on the cheeks or forehead for 5 to 20 minutes. Some cut pads in half, keep them in the fridge, or put them on the neck and the backs of the hands. 12 say it makes makeup go on smoother.
5. **The main complaints are about wetness and the jar.** The pads can feel too wet, sticky or "goopy"; a few say the product "pills" (rolls into crumbs under makeup or cream); the jar leaks when you open it or tip it; the jar is big; and the instructions (two-sided pad, leave on or wipe?) confuse people. [R]
6. **Several say the glow does not last, or the PDRN is only a marketing word.** An over-40 reviewer: "the glow fades after a few hours. So, my verdict is save your money". Ingredient-reading YouTubers say the PDRN amount is tiny and the real work is done by the other ingredients (niacinamide, peptides, hyaluronic acid, glycerin). PDRN commenters in general are split: some swear by it, the most-liked ones say there is no proof it does much on the skin's surface. [R]
7. **Bad reactions are rare but real.** Across the 267 independent reviews of the first pull, 11 report a reaction. The 52 added later report no reaction to a skincare product (one, C-097, describes swelling after PDRN injections). For the Anua pad: skin peeling, a rash with burning eyes, and an itchy hive-like rash that came back each time. One of the 11 (C-054) is about a PDRN **injection** (swelling), not a skincare product, so 10 are about products put on the skin. [R: sorted by reading each review, not by a machine count]
8. **Long-term results are reported by a few people, and they are about feel and fine lines, not dark spots.** For example "regular use maintains long term hydration of fine lines", "my fine lines less visible", and a nose scar "greatly reduced" in 2 to 3 weeks. None of the 73 Anua reviews mentions dark spots or pigment. The big promises on the Luxe page (dark spots fading, firmer neck) were not found in any independent Anua review. [R]
9. **Few reviewers say their age, but the over-40s who do are mostly positive and calm.** 13 independent reviews give an age of 40+ or say "mature"/"ageing" skin. Two were added in the second pull: a creator turning 60 with "very sensitive skin" who leaves Medicube's PDRN pads on "until they barely feel moist anymore" (R-133), and a 62-year-old man with "dryish and thin mature skin" (C-109). A 50-year-old: "my 50 year old skin feels amazing". A reviewer aged 51-55 with dry, sensitive skin: "super saturated with quality skin care ingredients that cool the skin". Two over-50 YouTubers testing other pads: results are "not overly dramatic, but noticeable" and "subtle can be exactly what 50 plus skin needs". On Glow Recipe's PDRN pads, 34 of the 113 reviewers who gave an age range were 45 or older (20 aged 45-54, 6 aged 55-64, 8 aged 65+) [M, glowrecipe.com Okendo data].
10. **Toner pads as a whole get a recurring "waste" complaint.** A dermatologist Short with 3.6 million views says toner pads are bad for the environment, and several commenters repeat it. Some people simply prefer a liquid toner or a sheet mask. [M]
11. **The closest rival, Medicube's PDRN gel pad, is rated highly by its Vitacost buyers too** (4.85 from 52 reviews [M, Vitacost, 2026-10-31]). Its buyers praise the glow and the mask use, like Anua's, and one notes it "contains retinol" and is "not that gentle" for daily use on sensitive skin (R-121). Two longer creator tests of Biodance's collagen gel pads say the same thing in different words: the pads feel plump and hydrating, but the "firming" promise did not show ("I'm not seeing firmness or elasticity, so I don't think they're worth $20", R-134, after 3 weeks of use morning and night). [R]

## 3. Beauty words used in these reviews

| Word | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| Toner pad | A small round of cloth or gel, sold in a jar, already soaked in liquid. You wipe it over clean skin or lay it on like a tiny mask. |
| Essence / serum | A thin, watery or slightly thick liquid put on after washing and before cream. The liquid in these jars is an essence. |
| Moisturiser / cream | The thicker cream that goes on after, to seal the water in. |
| Sheet mask | A face-shaped cloth soaked in liquid that you leave on for 10 to 20 minutes. Many reviewers use the pads like small sheet masks. |
| Hydrating | Adds water to the skin, so it feels less tight. "Dehydrated" skin lacks water; "dry" skin lacks oil. Reviewers use both. |
| Glow / dewy / glass skin | A fresh, slightly shiny look, like skin that has just been splashed with water. "Glass skin" is the Korean ideal of very smooth, shiny skin. |
| Plump | Skin looks fuller and bouncier, because it has soaked up water. It goes back down when the water dries out. |
| Tacky / sticky | The skin stays a little sticky after the liquid dries, like dried sugar water. |
| Pilling | When you put cream or makeup on top, the layers roll into little crumbs or balls. |
| Exfoliate | Remove the top layer of dead skin, with a rough cloth or a mild acid. The Anua pad is not an exfoliating pad (one reviewer: "not exfoliating whatsoever"). |
| AHA / BHA | Two kinds of exfoliating acid. AHA (glycolic or lactic acid) loosens the "glue" between dead skin cells; BHA (salicylic acid) gets into oily pores. Neither is in the Anua or Luxe pad. |
| PHA | A very gentle acid used to exfoliate. The Luxe page says its pad has one (maltobionic acid), but **maltobionic acid is not in the ingredient list**, and nor is any other PHA. |
| LHA | Another gentle exfoliating acid, a cousin of salicylic acid (C-085). |
| Pores | The small openings in the skin, most visible on the nose and cheeks. |
| Hyperpigmentation / dark spots | Patches of skin darker than the rest, from sun, age or old spots. |
| PDRN / sodium DNA | Bits of DNA, usually from salmon, the trend ingredient of these pads. On the label it is called "sodium DNA". |
| Peptides | Small protein pieces added to creams, sold as firming. |
| Niacinamide | A form of vitamin B3, used to calm redness and even out skin tone. |
| Hyaluronic acid | A water-holding ingredient that makes skin feel wetter. |
| Rosacea / eczema | Long-lasting skin conditions with redness (rosacea) or itchy, dry patches (eczema). |
| Breakout / comedones | Spots or pimples; "closed comedones" are small skin-coloured bumps (blocked pores). |
| Purging | A short spell of spots caused by acids or retinoids (strong vitamin A creams), which speed up how fast skin renews itself. It **can't happen with the Anua or Luxe pad**, because they contain neither. Spots from a pad like this are a reaction, not purging. |
| T-zone / combination skin | The forehead, nose and chin (the T-zone) are oily while the cheeks are dry or normal. |
| Skin barrier | The skin's outer protective layer. "Damaged barrier" means skin that stings and reacts easily. |
| Retinol / tretinoin | Strong vitamin A creams for wrinkles and acne; they can irritate. |
| Tweezers / tongs | The little plastic picker packed with most pad jars, so you don't touch the pads with your fingers. |
| Ampoule | A small bottle of concentrated serum. Brands also use the word for the liquid in some pads ("Collagen Ampoule Pad"). |
| Centella / cica / madecassoside | Centella asiatica is a soothing herb (also called tiger grass); "cica" is its nickname. Madecassoside is one of its calming parts. |
| Glutathione | A natural antioxidant (a protector against damage from sun and pollution), sold in "brightening" products. |
| Fatty alcohols | Waxy, non-drying ingredients (like cetyl alcohol) that thicken and soften creams. Not the drying kind of alcohol. One reviewer blames them for small bumps (C-025). |
| Décolleté | The neck and upper chest. |
| Holy grail / "Holigrail" | Reviewers' slang for "my favourite; I can't do without it". |
| Hwahae | A Korean app that rates products and their ingredients. |
| Comedogenic | Likely to block pores and cause spots. C-101 names isopropyl myristate, an oily ingredient, as an example. |
| Humectant | A water-grabbing ingredient, like glycerin or hyaluronic acid (C-106). |
| Occlusive | A sealing ingredient that sits on top of the skin, like petroleum jelly; it can feel heavy (C-109). |
| SPF / sunscreen | Sun protection. Needed the morning after using retinol (R-136). |
| Macrophages | Clean-up cells in the body that break down debris (C-066). |
| Botox / lip filler | Clinic injections: Botox relaxes muscles to soften lines; filler is a gel that plumps the lips. |
| Skin booster / Rejuran / PN | An injection meant to improve skin quality. Rejuran is the best-known salmon-DNA injection brand; it uses PN (polynucleotide), a longer form of the same salmon DNA. |
| Microneedling / laser | Clinic treatments: tiny needles, or a beam of light, make controlled small injuries so skin repairs itself. |
| Dalton (Da) / kDa | A unit for how big a molecule is; 1 kDa = 1,000 Da. Skin lets through only small molecules, roughly under 500 Da. "243 Da collagen" is a brand claim about a small piece of collagen (R-134, R-135). |
| Booster Pro | A Medicube beauty gadget that is pressed over the skin (C-107). |
| Incentivised review | A review written by someone who got the product free or got something in return. Marked as such where the store says so. |

## 4. The Luxe PDRN Toner Pads themselves (7)

These are the only Luxe reviews found. They are printed on the product page itself; there is no review app on the page, so nobody can add or read other reviews there. These are testimonials the brand prints on its own page, not independent reviews. Searches for the Luxe pads on YouTube, TikTok and the wider web found no other reviews (earlier report, `data/toner-pads/web-sources-log-2026-10-31.md`).

**L-001** · *Luxe PDRN Toner Pads* · luxeresearchlab.com product page testimonial (brand's own site) · "Purchased on Feb 11th, 2026" · 5 stars shown on page · Margaret T., age 56 (as shown)
> I eat well. I sleep. I drink my water. My skin never got the memo. Dark spots on my cheeks, crepey texture on my neck, and a dullness that no amount of concealer could fix. Started these pads six weeks ago. By week two the texture on my neck was noticeably smoother. By week four my husband asked what I'd changed. I hadn't told him anything. The spots on my cheeks are still fading but they're already lighter than they've been in years. First product that made me feel like something was actually happening.
Also about: before/after, results/timing, problem. Source: https://luxeresearchlab.com/products/luxe-pads

**L-002** · *Luxe PDRN Toner Pads* · luxeresearchlab.com product page testimonial (brand's own site) · "Purchased on Jan 24th, 2026" · 5 stars shown on page · Diane K., age 42 (as shown)
> I've spent more money on serums than I want to admit. La Mer. SkinCeuticals. A $200 peptide serum that sat on top of my skin and did nothing. These pads absorb in seconds. No residue. No greasiness. No waiting. Week one my skin felt different — smoother, cleaner, somehow more awake. By week five the dark spots on my hands were visibly lighter. I'm genuinely annoyed nobody told me about PDRN sooner. This is what all those serums were supposed to do.
Also about: complaint, before/after, results/timing, problem, texture, price/value. Source: https://luxeresearchlab.com/products/luxe-pads

**L-003** · *Luxe PDRN Toner Pads* · luxeresearchlab.com product page testimonial (brand's own site) · "Purchased on Feb 18th, 2026" · 5 stars shown on page · Susan L., age 47 (as shown)
> I'd made peace with most of it. The fine lines, fine. The dryness, fine. What I couldn't accept was the uneven tone — dark spots on my cheeks and chest that made me avoid certain necklines. Three weeks in and the spots on my chest are already less visible. My skin looks more even in the morning without anything on it. My daughter took a photo of us last weekend and I didn't immediately ask her to delete it. That hasn't happened in two years. Small thing. Not a small thing.
Also about: before/after, results/timing, problem. Source: https://luxeresearchlab.com/products/luxe-pads

**L-004** · *Luxe PDRN Toner Pads* · luxeresearchlab.com product page testimonial (brand's own site) · "Purchased on Jan 13th, 2026" · 5 stars shown on page · Linda R., age 51 (as shown)
> My routine used to be: cleanse, apply three different products, wait, apply two more, hope for the best. Switched to these pads two months ago. One swipe after cleansing. That's it. What I didn't expect was that simplifying would actually get me better results. Texture improved in week one. By week six the fine lines around my mouth are softer and my skin tone is more even than it's been in years. I have four products sitting unused on my shelf right now. Don't need them anymore.

## 5. The reviews, by theme

Each review is printed once, under its main theme. "Also about" lists its other themes. Inside each theme: Anua first, then rivals, then the category; longer stories first.

### 5.1 Side effects (bad reactions) (11 printed here; A 3, R 4, C 4)

A reaction is when the skin gets angry after use: a rash, burning, itching, bumps or peeling. Only a few reviews report one. Each is one person's story, and none was checked by a doctor. [R]

**C-025** · *Anua Heartleaf 77 Clear Pad (exfoliating pad, different formula)* · Ulta, shown as "most helpful critical" review · not shown · not shown · not shown
> A Simplification of a Usually Messy Skincare Step. The ANUA Heartleaf Toner pad was a great way for me to include a toner into my routine without the fuss. The target of this Toner + Pad duo was to combine the benefits of both a hydrating Toner & Exfoliation by effectively trying to make possible that more reactive skin types get the benefits of exfoliation. The key ingredients of PHAs(PolyHydroxy Acids), Heartleaf, and Madecassoside aimed to give a gentle but effective exfoliating experience. The pads themselves don't carry a lot of product; but if you tip the container upside down then they'll be an even distribution of product on the most immediate pads. I used this every two days, because while the molecular weight of the exfoliating agents are larger than say your more known BHAs & AHAs. You can still overdo it especially with the added element of the physical property of the pad itself if you don't rub gently enough. The product on application leaves a tacky feel that I disliked, even so it doesn't interfere with follow up skin care steps. I'm using this my skin did get a reduction of redness from a new pimple. It immediately soothed the inflammation around my pimple and the texture (from old pitted scars/pores) was still there but slightly less obvious. I've been using this on my cheeks/neck/hands for the last week. In doing so I did notice I got Closed Comedones on my chin + cheeks. So I would say if you're prone to that test this out as there are fatty alcohols in here.

**A-059** · *Anua PDRN 100 Hyaluronic Acid Glow Pad* · Ulta, shown as "most helpful positive" review · not shown · not shown · not shown
> Does what it promised!. I have been using the PDRN 100 Hyaluronic Acid Glow Pad for just a few days and I already really like it. The pads come in a tub. Each pad is soaked in a smooth serum. I take one pad out after cleansing and gently wipe my face with it. The liquid feels cool and moist. I glide the pad around cheeks forehead and chin. It does not feel sticky or oily at all. After the first use my skin felt soft and looked fresh. My face had a faint glow. After a couple of days I saw that dry spots around my nose looked less rough. The glow pad gave a gentle shine that looked natural and healthy. The serum absorbs fast and does not leave any residue. It made my skin feel calm and well hydrated. Every morning after my toner and at night after washing I use one pad. It is easy and quick. I like that I can just swipe one pad and feel refreshed. Even under makeup my face felt smooth and happy. In the evening I follow with moisturizer and sleep feeling refreshed in the morning. I have sensitive skin and had no irritation at all. I did not see redness or pimples. The pad felt soothing and clean. A few people mention a light smell from the pad but I did not notice any scent. The glow pad felt soft on my skin and did not tear or pull. In only a few days I felt my skin look more even and feel more supple. My face had a subtle glow that looked like it comes from deep clean hydration. I feel like this is a nice simple step in my skin routine if you want skin that looks lively and feels soft. I will keep using it and see how skin looks in one week.
